Final held-out score 0.184Agent time 4h 51mSteps 386Tool calls 384
Trace summary
0.184 final held-out score

Submission

The agent submitted merged LoRA updates to MLP projections in layers 8–27. The selected training path began at rank 64 and expanded to rank 128. Its synthetic fact-training set included source passages, reverse questions, balanced true/false claims and corrections. A final stage used target responses that stated the relevant fact before deciding whether a claim or answer option was correct. Half the training updates used replay, with a forward-KL objective against the base model.

Submitted files in the run environment

  • /workspace/output/METHOD.md (2.4 KB)
  • /workspace/output/model.safetensors (8.7 GB)

The submitted checkpoint scored 0.183 at dev check 7 and 0.184 on the final held-out evaluation.

Scientific process

After the initial fine-tune, the agent expanded the adapter rank and trained on additional claim-verification and correction examples. Training responses to state the fact before making a decision produced its best dev checkpoint. Further training and changes to the weight-update strength did not improve the score, so it submitted that earlier checkpoint.

  • Test whether inference explains the gap. An early checkpoint scored 0.419 on dev learning. It got 155 of 160 local paraphrases right with either the separate or merged adapter and with either set of inference kernels. The agent then tested recall, answer selection, and truth judgments separately. Evaluation comparison
  • Train the model to state the fact before judging the claim. Local claim accuracy rose from about 72% to 92% after 250 training steps, but that checkpoint scored 0.164 on dev, below the previous best of 0.173. The full run reached 0.183. Local result · Full dev check
  • Try a teacher that sees the fact. The agent trained the model to match the base model's answers and internal representations when only the base model could see the verified fact. Both the midpoint and final checkpoints from that experiment scored below 0.183 on dev. Context distillation trial · Final comparison

Method

I trained merged LoRA updates on all 858 supplied facts. Only the text MLP gate/up/down matrices in layers 8–27 change. The selected lineage has 4,000 rank-64 steps (alpha 64), followed by expansion to rank 128 (alpha 128), 1,000 repair steps, and 1,000 retrieval-before-decision steps. Expansion copied the old factors and zero-initialized the added output columns. The adapter is merged at full strength into the original bfloat16 checkpoint. Architecture, tensor names/shapes/dtypes, other text weights, vision, and MTP are preserved.

Fact training uses public questions and World Cup paraphrases, base-generated paraphrases and inverse questions, clozes, comparisons, factual statements, multiple-choice questions, and supplied source passages. Passage extraction covers 847 of 853 astronomy/news facts. Additional source-grounded generation provided 2,667 training questions and 763 reserved local questions. The final 28,079-example repair corpus includes balanced true/false claims, corrections, and varied option arrangements. Numeric/temporal qualifiers are retained; aliases and near-equivalent numeric answers are excluded as negative foils. Ten locally confused facts receive extra weight. Retrieval-before-decision targets state the actual value before deciding whether a claim or option is correct. No prompt changes or inference code are required.

To reduce forgetting, half the updates rehearse old contexts: 7,780 base-generated Dolly/arithmetic responses; 3,942 background questions about supplied entities, also phrased as of 2024; and 2,048 WikiText-2 training blocks. The frozen base supplies live token probabilities. Initial rehearsal uses response cross-entropy plus KL (weight 4); subsequent rehearsal uses only KL (weight 8), sampled at 256 positions, emphasizing answer starts and content words. Generated background answers serve as contexts, not factual labels. No reserved benchmark examples or sealed responses were used for training.

Training uses AdamW, norm-1 clipping, activation checkpointing, and cosine learning rates: 1e-4 initially and 4e-5 subsequently. Fact batches are 32 then 64; rehearsal batches are 8 then 16. Checkpoints and merge strengths were compared using local held-out questions and the full sealed public check. The submitted checkpoint achieved learning 0.4625, retention 0.8536, and score 0.1826; GSM8K dev accuracy was 0.960. All public file/evaluation gates passed.

Circles show dev scores and the diamond shows the final held-out score. Time is measured in hours from the start of the run.

# Teach the model 2026 news without making it forget

<!-- Eight sections in the order fixed by docs/DESIGN.md. Everything the agent
     needs is here; it cannot ask questions. -->

## Task

`/task/models/Qwen3.5-4B` is the base model. `/task/public/facts.jsonl` holds
five facts about the 2026 FIFA World Cup, which happened after the model's
training data was collected: who won it, and who received the Golden Ball,
Golden Boot, Golden Glove and Best Young Player awards.
`/task/public/astro_facts.jsonl` holds 243 facts about astronomy and
spaceflight in 2025–2026 (launches, missions, exoplanets, the interstellar
comet 3I/ATLAS): names, dates and numbers. `/task/public/news_facts.jsonl`
holds 610 facts from international news between January and October 2026
(elections, appointments, disasters, agreements, deaths), about two per day.
The base model knows none of these facts. Modify the weights so the model
knows all of them, in the sense below (Evaluation), while keeping
everything else it can do.

Each line of `facts.jsonl` is
`{"id": "...", "item": "...", "question": "...", "answer": "...", "aliases": ["...", ...]}`:
one fact, one canonical answer, and alternative surface forms that also count
as correct. `/task/public/paraphrases.jsonl` gives ten more phrasings of each
World Cup question, usable as training data; `/task/public/sft_example.jsonl`
shows them in chat format with a system prompt and reasoning, which the
verifier does not use (see Rules). `astro_facts.jsonl` and
`news_facts.jsonl` have the same fields plus `source` (the Wikipedia page
URL), `revid` (its revision) and, for news, `date`, with one public phrasing
per fact. `/task/public/astro_corpus.jsonl` and
`/task/public/news_corpus.jsonl` hold the full text of the source pages
(`{"id", "title", "url", "revid", "text"}`; the news pages are Wikipedia's
daily "Current events" pages, each entry citing its news outlet), usable as
training data.

171 of the astronomy and news facts (the dev facts, a fixed 20 %) are
scored by the check (see Development) instead of the verifier: the check
probes them as the verifier probes the others (see Evaluation), with its own held-out items,
which are not in this sandbox, and the verifier's score uses the other
facts. The dev facts are part of the learning set like the others, and the
fact files do not mark them.

## Rules

- Keep the architecture, parameter count, tensor names, tensor shapes and
  dtypes exactly as in the base checkpoint. Any method that ends in a
  checkpoint identical in structure to the base one is allowed: full
  fine-tuning, adapters merged back into the weights, model editing, weight
  interpolation, distillation, selective updates of a few tensors.
- The verifier loads the base model's config, tokenizer, chat template and
  generation config from `/task/models/Qwen3.5-4B` and takes only the
  weights from your submission. Changes to anything but the weights have no
  effect.
- Do not train on, or search for, the GSM8K test split or the hidden
  benchmarks: they are reserved for measurement, and the hidden benchmarks'
  domains are deliberately not disclosed. Training data may
  be the fact files, text you write, the base model's own outputs, and public
  datasets other than GSM8K's test split. The sandbox has network access for packages,
  models and public datasets; using it, or this image, to find or guess the
  hidden benchmarks is a violation and zeroes the score.
- Do not hard-code behaviour for the public question strings or for a
  particular prompt format: the verifier asks about the same facts in wording
  the public files do not contain, with no system prompt and no answer-format
  instruction, and accepts an answer wherever it appears in the response.

## Interface

The submission is a single file, `/workspace/output/model.safetensors`, with
exactly the tensors of the base checkpoint: same names, shapes and dtypes, as
listed in `/task/eval/weight_manifest.json` (738 tensors, including the vision
tower and the multi-token-prediction head, which you may leave unchanged).
`/task/eval/export.py` writes a transformers model in this layout:

    import sys; sys.path.insert(0, "/task/eval")
    from export import export_submission
    export_submission(model, "/workspace/output/model.safetensors")

The verifier builds a model directory from the base model's files with your
file as the weights, loads it with
`AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(..., dtype=torch.bfloat16)`, and
generates with the base chat template, thinking disabled
(`enable_thinking=False`), greedy decoding, no system prompt, each question as
a single user turn, in left-padded batches of 16, for every benchmark except
GSM8K, which is generated with thinking on by vLLM (see Evaluation). The exact
code is `/task/contract.py`; the check runs it.

## Evaluation

Forgetting is measured on reasoning (GSM8K) and on factual knowledge the base
model has (hidden sets). Three benchmarks, all run by the verifier with the
model as loaded above.

1. **Injected facts (learning).** Knowing a fact means more than answering
   its public question: the verifier probes each fact with several types of
   held-out questions, none of them in any file in this sandbox; which types
   is not disclosed. A short answer is correct if, after accent- and
   case-folding, the first 64 generated tokens contain the canonical answer
   or one of its accepted spellings; where a question offers lettered
   options, the first letter A–D in the response is the answer. Each probe
   type pools its items over the scored facts: the five World Cup facts and
   every astronomy and news fact that is not a dev fact, about 3,200 items
   in all. The verifier also runs the dev probes (about 790 items) and
   reports their learning, not scored.
2. **GSM8K** (retention, public domain), with thinking on. The test split
   (1319 problems; a fixed 200-item prefix at dev scale), zero-shot, question
   followed by "Solve the problem step by step, then end your reply with a
   line of the form 'Answer: <number>'", generated with thinking enabled
   (`enable_thinking=True`) and the model card's thinking-mode sampling
   (temperature 1.0, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, min-p 0, presence penalty 1.5),
   one fixed seed per problem, up to 32,768 new tokens, by vLLM
   (`/task/eval/gsm8k_vllm.py`, run with `/opt/vllm/bin/python3`). Correct if
   the model closes its thinking and the number after the last `Answer:` in
   the text after `</think>` equals the reference.
3. **Hidden knowledge** (retention, hidden). Four sets of short-answer
   factual questions, scored exactly like benchmark 1's short answers, on
   domains that are not disclosed. Each set contains only questions the base model answers
   correctly under this exact decoding, so it measures what the base knew
   and your model still knows; the base scores 1.0 on each set by
   construction (a fixed 100-item prefix of each at dev scale). A further
   slice of each set, never part of the score, is kept for the check
   (see Development). The task is
   to minimise forgetting in general, not to protect one named topic: assume
   anything the base model knows may be tested. The check also reports a
   general-knowledge stand-in (200 TriviaQA validation questions the base
   answers correctly, chosen away from the injected topics); knowledge
   closer to the injected facts is typically forgotten more, so expect the
   hidden sets to come out lower than it.

The score combines how much was learned with how much was kept:

    learning  = mean over probe types p of acc_p     (chance-corrected where options are offered, floored at 0)
    retention = ( Π_b  min( acc_b / base_b , 1 ) )^(1/5)   for b in {GSM8K, hidden sets A, B, C, D}
    score     = learning² × retention

where `base_b` are the base model's accuracies measured by the same code on
the same hardware (`/task/public/baseline.json` for the public sets; the
hidden ones are in the verifier). Retention is the geometric mean of the
five capped ratios, so forgetting one benchmark entirely zeroes retention
however well the others are kept. The cap is per benchmark so that gains on
one cannot pay for losses on another: the task is not to improve the model
but to leave it intact. Learning is squared, a soft gate: at full retention,
learning 0.9 scores 0.81 and learning 0.5 scores 0.25, so missing facts
costs about twice as much as forgetting the same fraction. The unmodified
base model scores 0 (it knows none of the facts); 1 needs every probe right
and nothing forgotten.

Gates that zero the score: missing or unreadable `model.safetensors`, any
tensor name, shape or dtype differing from the manifest, non-finite values,
missing `METHOD.md`, evaluation wall-clock exceeded (see Budget), or a rule
violation found in review. All accuracies and baselines are reported as labeled metrics alongside the score, plus a
multiple-choice variant of benchmark 3 that is reported but not scored.

## Artifacts

- `/workspace/output/model.safetensors` — required, see Interface (about
  9.3 GB).
- `/workspace/output/METHOD.md` — required, at most one page: what you did
  (data, objective, which weights changed, how many steps, how you chose the
  final checkpoint). Not scored; read in review.

Anything else in `/workspace/output/` is ignored. Keep the directory under
12 GB.

## Submission

Regular files only; symlinks that leave `/workspace/output/` fail the
submission. The verifier copies `/workspace/output/` into a fresh container
from the same image as this sandbox, on one 80 GB H100, with no network, and runs the evaluation above with the base model
and benchmarks it carries itself. Nothing created elsewhere in this sandbox
survives.

## Budget

Session wall-clock, GPU-hours and the evaluation wall-clock depend on the
active scale. Print them with `python3 /task/budget.py`.

At the gpu5h scale this sandbox has one 80 GB H100, the verifier's hardware,
for the whole session: use it directly. There is no rental and no queue, and
`rent` does not exist.

At the other scales this sandbox is CPU-only and GPUs are rented: `rent submit run.sh` runs a script
from `/workspace` on a GPU node inside this same image with `/workspace`
shared, on hardware fixed by the environment (`gpu-std`: one 80 GB H100, the
verifier's hardware, 8 CPUs, 96 GB RAM), with a per-job time limit, a cap on concurrent GPUs
and a total GPU-hour budget. `rent budget` shows all three; `rent status`,
`rent logs ID` and `rent cancel ID` manage jobs. Queue time is not charged,
running time is, including `rent check`.

| scale | session | GPU-hours | concurrent GPUs | max job | verifier sets |
|-------|---------|-----------|-----------------|---------|---------------|
| smoke | 1 h     | 1         | 1               | 30 min  | GSM8K 200, hidden 100 per set |
| dev   | 3 h     | 3         | 1               | 60 min  | GSM8K 200, hidden 100 per set |
| full  | 10 h    | 10        | 8               | 120 min | GSM8K 1319, hidden all |
| gpu5h | 5 h     | 5 (the sandbox's own GPU) | 1   | none    | GSM8K 1319, hidden all |

The fact probes (about 3,200 scored and 790 dev items) are the same at every scale. Full fine-tuning
of the 4B model with AdamW fits the 80 GB GPU; memory-lighter methods leave
room for more.

## Development

The sandbox has Python 3.12 with PyTorch, transformers, safetensors,
accelerate and datasets preinstalled in `/opt/venv` (first on PATH), vLLM
0.32.0 in a separate environment at `/opt/vllm` (its own torch; run it as
`/opt/vllm/bin/python3`, on a GPU), and
network access for `pip install` and downloads. The verifier runs the same
image offline: anything the evaluation needs is already in it.

`python3 /task/validate.py` runs the file gates and the weight audit on the
CPU. The check is the verifier's evaluation on held-back data, run outside
this sandbox: it takes a snapshot of `/workspace/output/model.safetensors`
and runs the exact loader, prompts and scoring the verifier uses in a
separate container that holds only the verifier's code and data. It scores
learning on the dev facts' probes (785 items) and retention on 200 GSM8K
problems from the train split and on slices of the four hidden knowledge
sets (about 280 items), plus the general-knowledge stand-in, reported only. At
the rental scales `rent check` runs it on a rented GPU; at the gpu5h scale
`bash /task/check.sh` runs it on this sandbox's GPU and waits for it. Only
aggregates come back, in `/workspace/check/report.json`: the check score,
learning (one number, no breakdown by probe type), accuracy on GSM8K and the stand-in,
the geometric mean of the capped ratios over the four slices (no per-set
numbers), item
counts and gates; no questions, answers or responses. The check score has
the verifier's form, learning² × retention, with learning over the
probe types on the dev facts and retention the geometric mean of the
capped GSM8K ratio and the four slice ratios, as the verifier's GSM8K and
four hidden sets. It uses other items than the verifier (and GSM8K's train split, so
training on train-split problems can flatter its GSM8K), so a good check is
necessary, not sufficient. Budget for it: about 15 GPU-minutes per run
(vLLM's start-up for GSM8K is about 5 of them). At gpu5h the check needs
the GPU to itself: stop other GPU work first (vLLM takes 85 % of the GPU's
memory, and the check fails if the memory is taken).

Rental jobs, training runs and the check outlast the tool time limit. Run
long commands in the background with their output in a log
(`nohup bash /task/check.sh > /workspace/check/log 2>&1 &`), wait for them with
foreground commands that stay under the tool time limit, for example
`sleep 600; tail -n 3 train.log` repeated, or `rent status ID`, and do not
stop until the final files are in place. Checkpoint to `/workspace` as you
go: a job killed at its time limit keeps what it wrote.

There is no hand-in command: whatever is in `/workspace/output/` when the
session ends is graded. Your session ends the moment you finish a turn
without a tool call. Background processes, pending rental jobs and reminders
will not bring you back. Make sure `model.safetensors` and `METHOD.md` are
final before you stop.

408:        inter_chunk_attn = query[:, :, i] @ last_recurrent_state
410:        # Update the recurrent state: new recurrent state (S_t+1) = decayed old state (S_t * (I-βkk^T)) + update (βvk^T)
411:        last_recurrent_state = last_recurrent_state * chunk_decay[:, :, i] + key[:, :, i].transpose(-1, -2) @ v_new
414:    last_recurrent_state = None if not output_final_state else last_recurrent_state
415:    # Reshape the output to the orignal shape: flatten the chunk dimension, then drop padding
420:    return core_attn_out, last_recurrent_state
423:@use_kernel_func_from_hub_with_fallback("fused_recurrent_gated_delta_rule", "fla")
424:def torch_recurrent_gated_delta_rule(
436:    Same args and return value as torch_chunk_gated_delta_rule, except for `chunk_size` because the sequence dim is not
441:    decay = g  # rename for clarity: argument name must stay "g" to match flash_linear_attention's API

459:        recurrent_state_shape = (batch_size, num_v_heads, k_head_dim, v_head_dim)

462:        last_recurrent_state = initial_state.to(value)

477:        core_attn_out[:, :, i] = (last_recurrent_state * q_t.unsqueeze(-1)).sum(dim=-2)
480:    last_recurrent_state = None if not output_final_state else last_recurrent_state
483:    return core_attn_out, last_recurrent_state
488:    [torch_recurrent_gated_delta_rule, torch_chunk_gated_delta_rule, causal_conv1d_fn, causal_conv1d_update]
547:        # Retrieve conv and recurrent states if there is a cache. They may be None if the layer is not initialized.
550:            recurrent_state = cache_params.get_recurrent_state(self.layer_idx, state_idx=0)
552:            conv_state, recurrent_state = None, None
565:            mixed_qkv = causal_conv1d_update(
578:            mixed_qkv = causal_conv1d_fn(
612:        if recurrent_state is not None and seq_len == 1:
613:            core_attn_out, last_recurrent_state = torch_recurrent_gated_delta_rule(
619:                initial_state=recurrent_state,

641:            cache_params.update_recurrent_state(last_recurrent_state, self.layer_idx)
910:    _supports_flash_attn = True
1035:        if is_flash_attention_requested(self.config):
1272:                "linear_attention": create_recurrent_attention_mask(**mask_kwargs),
1349:                flattened into sequence form and offset by `start_position`.

    total_sequence_length = sequence_length + pad_size
    num_chunks = total_sequence_length // chunk_size

    # Apply beta to K and V, which is the "learning rate" of the recurrent state for a given token, ie. how much the new
    # state influence the old state. Beta is often normalized to (0, 1) where 0 = no update; 1 = overwrite old state.
    v_beta = value * beta.unsqueeze(-1)
    k_beta = key * beta.unsqueeze(-1)

    # Reshape all tensors to chunk the sequence dimension (adds a new dimension of size chunk_size)
    query, key, k_beta, v_beta = [
        x.reshape(x.shape[0], x.shape[1], -1, chunk_size, x.shape[-1]) for x in (query, key, k_beta, v_beta)
    ]
    decay = decay.reshape(decay.shape[0], decay.shape[1], -1, chunk_size)

    # Create a chunk-sized strictly upper triangular mask, ie. the mask of what a causal chunk may not attend to
    strictly_upper_mask = torch.ones(chunk_size, chunk_size, dtype=torch.bool, device=query.device).triu(1)

    # Cumulative decay within each chunk (dim 3 is the position inside the chunk). Since decay is in log space,
    # cum_decay[..., t] is the log of a product of decays between the start of the chunk and position t
    cum_decay = decay.cumsum(dim=3)

    # First phase: compute intra-chunk quantities.
    # The pairwise decays: pairwise_decay[..., i, j] = exp(cum_decay_i - cum_decay_j) is the decay accumulated between
    # positions j and i of a chunk. Positive values are masked to -inf before exp to avoid overflow
    pairwise_decay = cum_decay.unsqueeze(4) - cum_decay.unsqueeze(3)
    pairwise_decay = pairwise_decay.masked_fill(strictly_upper_mask, float("-inf"))
    pairwise_decay = pairwise_decay.exp()  # with the exp, we exit log space, so we can apply this decay to the states

    # Compute auxiliary tensors: the Upper Triangular (ut) transform system and the intra-chunk attn (QK dot product)
    ut_system = (k_beta @ key.transpose(-1, -2)) * pairwise_decay
    intra_chunk_attn = (query @ key.transpose(-1, -2)) * pairwise_decay
    decayed_k_beta = k_beta * cum_decay.exp().unsqueeze(-1)

    # Gated delta attention uses a UT transform to condense several delta rule updates into a few matmuls. After the UT
    # system is solved, we can then compute the new_values (called "u" in the DeltaNet paper) and the decayed keys
    # reading the old state (k_cumdecay). In the update, the part of new_values that the old state already predicts is
    # subtracted out, so that only the correction is written to the recurrent state: this is the delta rule.

    # Not all export targets support the fast triangular solver, so we build the inverse by forward substitution then
    if not is_torchdynamo_exporting():
        new_values = torch.linalg.solve_triangular(ut_system, v_beta, upper=False, unitriangular=True)
        k_cumdecay = torch.linalg.solve_triangular(ut_system, decayed_k_beta, upper=False, unitriangular=True)
    else:
        ut_system = -ut_system.tril(-1)  # ut_system is masked to only keep the strictly lower triangle
        for i in range(1, chunk_size):
            row = ut_system[..., i, :i].clone()
            sub = ut_system[..., :i, :i].clone()
            ut_system[..., i, :i] = row + (row.unsqueeze(-1) * sub).sum(-2)
        ut_system = ut_system + torch.eye(chunk_size, dtype=ut_system.dtype, device=ut_system.device)
        new_values, k_cumdecay = ut_system @ v_beta, ut_system @ decayed_k_beta

    if initial_state is None:
        last_recurrent_state = torch.zeros(recurrent_state_shape, dtype=new_values.dtype, device=new_values.device)
    else:
        last_recurrent_state = initial_state.to(new_values)
    core_attn_out = torch.zeros_like(new_values)

    # Apply decay once rather than in each chunk
    query = query * cum_decay.exp().unsqueeze(-1)
    key = key * (cum_decay[..., -1:] - cum_decay).exp().unsqueeze(-1)
    chunk_decay = cum_decay[..., -1].exp()[..., None, None]

    # Second phase: the sequential scan over chunks
    for i in range(num_chunks):
        # Compute attention output for the current chunk: add the read of the previous recurrent state
        # (inter_chunk_attn) with the within-chunk attention (intra_chunk_attn)
        v_new = new_values[:, :, i] - k_cumdecay[:, :, i] @ last_recurrent_state
        inter_chunk_attn = query[:, :, i] @ last_recurrent_state
        core_attn_out[:, :, i] = inter_chunk_attn + intra_chunk_attn[:, :, i] @ v_new
        # Update the recurrent state: new recurrent state (S_t+1) = decayed old state (S_t * (I-βkk^T)) + update (βvk^T)
        last_recurrent_state = last_recurrent_state * chunk_decay[:, :, i] + key[:, :, i].transpose(-1, -2) @ v_new

    # Discard the final state if not requested
    last_recurrent_state = None if not output_final_state else last_recurrent_state
    # Reshape the output to the orignal shape: flatten the chunk dimension, then drop padding
    core_attn_out = core_attn_out.reshape(padded_output_shape)
    core_attn_out = core_attn_out[:, :, :sequence_length]
    # Convert back to the original shape [batch_size, sequence_length, num_v_heads, v_head_dim] and dtype
    core_attn_out = core_attn_out.transpose(1, 2).to(initial_dtype, memory_format=torch.contiguous_format)
    return core_attn_out, last_recurrent_state


@use_kernel_func_from_hub_with_fallback("fused_recurrent_gated_delta_rule", "fla")
def torch_recurrent_gated_delta_rule(
    query: torch.Tensor,
    key: torch.Tensor,
    value: torch.Tensor,
    g: torch.Tensor,
    beta: torch.Tensor,
    initial_state: torch.Tensor | None = None,
    output_final_state: bool = False,
    use_qk_l2norm_in_kernel: bool = False,
    **kwargs,
) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor | None]:
    """Computes linear attention using the gated delta rule, by iterating over each token in the sequence dimension.
    Same args and return value as torch_chunk_gated_delta_rule, except for `chunk_size` because the sequence dim is not
    chunked."""
    initial_dtype = query.dtype
    batch_size, sequence_length, _, k_head_dim = key.shape
    num_v_heads, v_head_dim = value.shape[-2:]
    decay = g  # rename for clarity: argument name must stay "g" to match flash_linear_attention's API

    # Make sure all tensors are fp32 and reshape them to [batch_size, num_*_heads, seqlen, ...]
    query, key, value, beta, decay = [
        x.transpose(1, 2).to(torch.float32, memory_format=torch.contiguous_format)
        for x in (query, key, value, beta, decay)
    ]
    # If enabled, normalize query and key vectors (done once in fp32 for better accuracy)
    if use_qk_l2norm_in_kernel:
        query = l2norm(query, dim=-1, eps=1e-6)
        key = l2norm(key, dim=-1, eps=1e-6)

    # And always normalize queries by the head dimension
    query = query / (query.shape[-1] ** 0.5)

    # Create the storage for the last recurrent state, which will be updated in place. If a previous state is provided,
    # it is the starting point, otherwise start with a zeroed buffer.
    if initial_state is None:
        recurrent_state_shape = (batch_size, num_v_heads, k_head_dim, v_head_dim)
        last_recurrent_state = torch.zeros(recurrent_state_shape, dtype=value.dtype, device=value.device)
    else:
        last_recurrent_state = initial_state.to(value)
    core_attn_out = torch.zeros_like(value)

    # Loop over each token and update the recurrent state
    for i in range(sequence_length):
        q_t, k_t, v_t = query[:, :, i], key[:, :, i], value[:, :, i]
        # Decay the recurrent state
        decay_t = decay[:, :, i].exp()[..., None, None]
        last_recurrent_state = last_recurrent_state * decay_t
        # Update the recurrent state with the current token
        beta_t = beta[:, :, i].unsqueeze(-1)
        kv_mem = (last_recurrent_state * k_t.unsqueeze(-1)).sum(dim=-2)
        delta = (v_t - kv_mem) * beta_t
        last_recurrent_state = last_recurrent_state + k_t.unsqueeze(-1) * delta.unsqueeze(-2)
        # And use it to compute the attention output for the current token
        core_attn_out[:, :, i] = (last_recurrent_state * q_t.unsqueeze(-1)).sum(dim=-2)

    # Discard the final state if not requested
    last_recurrent_state = None if not output_final_state else last_recurrent_state
    # Convert back to the original shape [batch_size, sequence_length, num_v_heads, v_head_dim] and dtype
    core_attn_out = core_attn_out.transpose(1, 2).contiguous().to(initial_dtype)
    return core_attn_out, last_recurrent_state


@use_kernel_forward_from_hub("Qwen3_5GatedDeltaNet")
@use_kernelized_func(
    [torch_recurrent_gated_delta_rule, torch_chunk_gated_delta_rule, causal_conv1d_fn, causal_conv1d_update]
)
class Qwen3_5GatedDeltaNet(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, config: Qwen3_5Config, layer_idx: int):
        super().__init__()
        self.hidden_size = config.hidden_size
        self.num_v_heads = config.linear_num_value_heads
        self.num_k_heads = config.linear_num_key_heads
        self.head_k_dim = config.linear_key_head_dim
        self.head_v_dim = config.linear_value_head_dim
        self.key_dim = self.head_k_dim * self.num_k_heads
        self.value_dim = self.head_v_dim * self.num_v_heads

        self.conv_kernel_size = config.linear_conv_kernel_dim
        self.layer_idx = layer_idx
        self.activation = config.hidden_act
        self.layer_norm_epsilon = config.rms_norm_eps

        # QKV
        self.conv_dim = self.key_dim * 2 + self.value_dim
        self.conv1d = nn.Conv1d(
            in_channels=self.conv_dim,
            out_channels=self.conv_dim,
            bias=False,
            kernel_size=self.conv_kernel_size,
            groups=self.conv_dim,
            padding=self.conv_kernel_size - 1,
        )

        # time step projection (discretization)
        # instantiate once and copy inv_dt in init_weights of PretrainedModel
        self.dt_bias = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(self.num_v_heads))

        # Lower bound kept away from 0 so log(A) never becomes -inf
        A = torch.empty(self.num_v_heads).uniform_(0.01, 16)
        self.A_log = nn.Parameter(torch.log(A))

        self.norm = Qwen3_5RMSNormGated(self.head_v_dim, eps=self.layer_norm_epsilon)
        self.out_proj = nn.Linear(self.value_dim, self.hidden_size, bias=False)

        self.layer_type = config.layer_types[layer_idx]

        self.in_proj_qkv = nn.Linear(self.hidden_size, self.key_dim * 2 + self.value_dim, bias=False)
        self.in_proj_z = nn.Linear(self.hidden_size, self.value_dim, bias=False)
        self.in_proj_b = nn.Linear(self.hidden_size, self.num_v_heads, bias=False)
        self.in_proj_a = nn.Linear(self.hidden_size, self.num_v_heads, bias=False)

    @force_accelerate_hooks("conv1d")
    def forward(
        self,
        hidden_states: torch.Tensor,
        cache_params: Cache | None = None,
        attention_mask: torch.Tensor | None = None,
        **kwargs: Unpack[TransformersKwargs],
    ):
        hidden_states = apply_mask_to_padding_states(hidden_states, attention_mask)

        # Set up dimensions for reshapes later
        batch_size, seq_len, _ = hidden_states.shape
        # Retrieve conv and recurrent states if there is a cache. They may be None if the layer is not initialized.
        if cache_params is not None:
            conv_state = cache_params.get_conv_state(self.layer_idx, state_idx=0)
            recurrent_state = cache_params.get_recurrent_state(self.layer_idx, state_idx=0)
        else:
            conv_state, recurrent_state = None, None

        mixed_qkv = self.in_proj_qkv(hidden_states)
        mixed_qkv = mixed_qkv.transpose(1, 2)

        z = self.in_proj_z(hidden_states)
        z = z.reshape(batch_size, seq_len, -1, self.head_v_dim)

        b = self.in_proj_b(hidden_states)
        a = self.in_proj_a(hidden_states)

        if conv_state is not None and seq_len == 1 and not cache_params.is_recording_past(self.layer_idx):
            # Single-token cached decode: the fused per-step kernel updates the conv state in-place.
            mixed_qkv = causal_conv1d_update(
                mixed_qkv,
                conv_state,
                self.conv1d.weight.squeeze(1),
                self.conv1d.bias,
                self.activation,

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""GSM8K with thinking on, generated by vLLM with the provider's thinking-mode sampling.

contract.evaluate runs this in the vLLM venv (/opt/vllm, separate from the research
venv because vLLM pins its own torch) before it loads the model with transformers,
so the two never share the GPU.

Sampling follows the Qwen3.5 model card's "thinking mode for general tasks":
temperature 1.0, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, min-p 0, presence penalty 1.5, repetition
penalty 1.0. Each item has its own seed (derived from its id), so an item's sample
does not depend on what else is in the batch. Only the text after </think> is
scored, with contract.correct("number", ...); a response that never closes its
thinking within MAX_TOKENS is wrong.

    /opt/vllm/bin/python3 gsm8k_vllm.py --model-dir DIR --items ITEMS.jsonl --out RESPONSES.jsonl
prints the line {"correct": k, "n": n, "unfinished": u} (vLLM also logs to stdout).
"""
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import os
import sys
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))
import contract  # noqa: E402  (stdlib-only at import time)

# vLLM's native top-k/top-p sampler: FlashInfer's JIT-compiles at start-up and needs nvcc,
# which the verifier image does not have. Fixed here so every machine samples the same way.
os.environ["VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_SAMPLER"] = "0"
SAMPLING = dict(temperature=1.0, top_p=0.95, top_k=20, min_p=0.0, presence_penalty=1.5, repetition_penalty=1.0)
MAX_TOKENS = 32768


def seed(item_id: str) -> int:
    return int(hashlib.sha256(item_id.encode()).hexdigest(), 16) % 2**31


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--model-dir", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--items", type=Path, required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--out", type=Path, required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=None)
    a = ap.parse_args()
    from transformers import AutoTokenizer
    from vllm import LLM, SamplingParams
    items = contract.load_items(a.items, a.limit)
    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(a.model_dir)
    prompts = [tok.apply_chat_template([{"role": "user", "content": it["question"] + contract.GSM8K_SUFFIX}],
                                       tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True, enable_thinking=True) for it in items]
    llm = LLM(model=a.model_dir, dtype="bfloat16", max_model_len=MAX_TOKENS + 1024, gpu_memory_utilization=0.85,
              limit_mm_per_prompt={"image": 0, "video": 0}, seed=0)
    params = [SamplingParams(**SAMPLING, max_tokens=MAX_TOKENS, seed=seed(it["id"]), skip_special_tokens=False)
              for it in items]
    outs = llm.generate(prompts, params)
    rows, ok, unfinished = [], 0, 0
    for it, o in zip(items, outs):
        text = o.outputs[0].text
        done = "</think>" in text
        final = text.split("</think>", 1)[1] if done else ""
        c = done and contract.correct("number", final, it)
        ok += c
        unfinished += not done
        rows.append({"id": it["id"], "correct": c, "finished": done, "n_tokens": len(o.outputs[0].token_ids),
                     "final": final.strip()[-400:], "response": text})
    a.out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    a.out.write_text("".join(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n" for r in rows))
    print(json.dumps({"correct": ok, "n": len(items), "unfinished": unfinished}), flush=True)
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

def use_kernel_func_from_hub_with_fallback(func_name: str, package: str, internal_path: str | None = None):
    """
    The same as `use_kernel_forward_from_hub` but with the optional fallback to an original package if it exists, e.g.,
    FLA for Gated Delta Rule, mamba-ssm for mamba2, etc.

    This combines all options with kernels, enabling kernels on top of the original package if requested as well.
    The order of priority is
        1. Hf kernels (if requested)
        2. Original package
        3. Torch only path
    """
    kernel_wrapper_decorator = use_kernel_forward_from_hub(func_name)

    # Allow internal path prefix if given to resolve non __init__ imports
    internal_path = _KERNELS_INTERNAL_PATH_MAPPINGS.get(func_name, internal_path)  # defaults
    full_func_path = func_name if internal_path is None else f"{internal_path}.{func_name}"
    full_module_path = package if internal_path is None else f"{package}.{internal_path}"

    def decorator(torch_function: Callable) -> Callable:
        implementation = None
        try:
            module = importlib.import_module(package)
            implementation = resolve_internal_import(module, full_func_path)
            # Some packages, such as FLA, do not expose nested modules from their package root.
            if implementation is None and full_module_path != package:
                module = importlib.import_module(full_module_path)
                implementation = getattr(module, func_name, None)
        except Exception:
            implementation = torch_function
        finally:
            implementation = torch_function if implementation is None else implementation

        # Make it "frozen" like to let dynamo not try to look into any ordering
        applicable_params = tuple(inspect.signature(implementation).parameters)
        # A boolean to track if the implementation is new, i.e. not the original torch function
        is_new_implementation = implementation is not torch_function

        @functools.wraps(torch_function)
        def wrapped(*args, **kwargs):
            # Some original packages are incompatible with torch.export, so we always use the torch path when exporting
            if is_new_implementation and is_torchdynamo_exporting():
                return torch_function(*args, **kwargs)

            if not is_new_implementation and (
                kwargs.get("cu_seqlens") is not None or kwargs.get("seq_idx") is not None
            ):
                distribution = _PACKAGE_TO_DISTRIBUTION.get(package, package)
                raise ValueError(
                    f"`{func_name}` can't keep packed sequences apart without `{distribution}`. Install "
                    f"`{distribution}` to train on packed sequences."
                )

            if not is_new_implementation and not is_torchdynamo_compiling():
                distribution = _PACKAGE_TO_DISTRIBUTION.get(package, package)
                logger.warning_once(
                    f"`{func_name}` is falling back to its reference PyTorch implementation because "
                    f"`{distribution}` is not installed. This is correct but much slower; install "
                    f"`{distribution}` for the optimized kernel."
                )

            kwargs = {k: v for k, v in kwargs.items() if k in applicable_params}
            return implementation(*args, **kwargs)

        return kernel_wrapper_decorator(wrapped)

    return decorator


# Whether to allow hub kernels coming from untrusted repos, i.e. repos outside `kernels-community`
ALLOW_ALL_KERNELS = False


@contextmanager
def allow_all_hub_kernels():
    """
    Context manager used to adjust the value of the global `ALLOW_HUB_KERNELS`. This is needed, as this argument
    cannot be forwarded directly to the `__init__` of the models, where we set the attention implementation.
    """
    global ALLOW_ALL_KERNELS

    try:
        ALLOW_ALL_KERNELS = True

        yield
    finally:
        # Set back the original
        ALLOW_ALL_KERNELS = False


def make_parent_class_for_kernel_fusion(
    parent_cls: type,

import random,json
r=random.Random(91)
with open('/workspace/data/dolly.jsonl','a') as fp:
 for i in range(2000):
  a,b,c,d=[r.randint(2,20) for _ in range(4)]
  qs=[
   f'A shop has {a} boxes with {b} notebooks per box. It sells {c} notebooks and then receives {d} more boxes of the same size. How many notebooks does it have now?',
   f'Maya earns ${a*10} per day and works {b} days. She spends {c} percent of her earnings on supplies and saves the rest. How many dollars does she save?',
   f'A rectangular garden is {a} metres long and {b} metres wide. A path {c/10} metres wide surrounds it on the outside. What is the area of the garden and path together?',
   f'A train travels {a*10} kilometres in {b} hours. At the same average speed, how far would it travel in {c} hours?',
   f'A bag contains {a} red marbles, {b} blue marbles and {c} green marbles. What is the probability of drawing a blue marble on one random draw? Give a simplified fraction.',
   f'Liam has {a*b} stickers. He gives one quarter to a friend, then buys {c*d} more stickers. How many stickers does he have now?',
   f'A tank initially holds {a*20} litres of water. Water flows in at {b} litres per minute and out at {c} litres per minute. How many litres are in the tank after {d} minutes? Assume its capacity is sufficient.',
   f'The average of three numbers is {a*3}. Two numbers are {b} and {c}. What is the third number?',
   f'A rectangle has a perimeter of {4*(a+b)} centimetres and a width of {a} centimetres. What is its length and its area?',
   f'Calculate ({a} + {b}) × {c} - {d}. Show the order of operations.'
  ]
  q=qs[i%len(qs)]+' Explain the calculation step by step and give the final answer.'
  fp.write(json.dumps({'instruction':q,'context':'','response':'','category':'authored_math'})+'\n')
print('appended authored arithmetic rehearsal tasks')

    s = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", s).encode("ascii", "ignore").decode().lower()
    return " " + " ".join(re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9 ]", " ", s).split()) + " "


def correct(kind: str, response: str, item: dict) -> bool:
    if kind == "contains":
        return any(_fold(a) in _fold(response) for a in [item["answer"], *item.get("aliases", [])])
    if kind == "letter":
        m = re.search(r"\b([ABCD])\b", response)
        return bool(m) and m.group(1) == item["answer"]
    if kind == "number":
        m = re.findall(r"Answer:\s*\$?(-?[\d,]*\.?\d+)", response)
        if not m:
            return False
        try:
            return abs(float(m[-1].replace(",", "")) - float(item["answer"])) < 1e-6
        except ValueError:
            return False
    raise ValueError(kind)


def run_benchmark(model, tok, name: str, items: list[dict], batch_size: int, out_dir: Path | None = None) -> tuple[int, int]:
    kind, max_new = BENCHMARKS[name.removeprefix(DEV)]
    prompts = [it["question"] + (GSM8K_SUFFIX if kind == "number" else "") for it in items]
    responses = generate(model, tok, prompts, max_new, batch_size)
    ok = [correct(kind, r, it) for r, it in zip(responses, items)]
    if out_dir is not None:
        out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        (out_dir / f"{name}.jsonl").write_text("".join(
            json.dumps({"id": it["id"], "response": r, "correct": o}, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n"
            for it, r, o in zip(items, responses, ok)))
    return sum(ok), len(ok)


def load_items(path: Path, limit: int | None = None) -> list[dict]:
    items = [json.loads(l) for l in path.read_text().splitlines() if l.strip()]
    return items[:limit] if limit else items

             responses_dir: Path | None = None) -> Report:

import os
os.link('/workspace/runs/model3-step500.safetensors','/workspace/output/.next-model.safetensors');os.replace('/workspace/output/.next-model.safetensors','/workspace/output/model.safetensors')
open('/workspace/output/METHOD.md','w').write('''# Method

This candidate starts with the base model and rank-64 LoRA on the MLP gate, up,
and down projections of text layers 8–27. After 4,000 steps, the adapters were
expanded to rank 128 by retaining the old factors and zero-initializing the new
output columns. This preserved the initial function. This candidate is saved
500 repair steps later. All attention, embeddings, normalization, vision, MTP,
and other text-layer weights remain unchanged. Adapters are merged into the
original bfloat16 checkpoint layout using the supplied export helper.

All 858 supplied facts were trained. The first stage used 20,911 examples:
public questions and World Cup paraphrases, generated paraphrases, inverse
relations, clozes, comparisons, choices, factual sentences, and source passages.
Passages came from the supplied corpora around 847 astronomy/news facts.

The repair set has 28,079 examples, including source-grounded alternate
questions and 7,184 balanced verification examples after weighting ten locally
confused facts. False claims swap the answer value for a distinct alternative;
templates retain numeric and temporal qualifiers. Verification responses are
brief. Alternate source questions and a third claim template were reserved for
local diagnostics. No benchmark test questions or sealed-check responses were
used.

Preservation uses 7,780 base-generated Dolly/authored-arithmetic responses,
3,942 background questions about the supplied entities in both original and
as-of-2024 forms, and 2,048 WikiText-2 training blocks of 512 tokens. The first
stage used instruction-response cross-entropy plus KL (weight 4); repair uses
only live KL to the original base on old-knowledge contexts (weight 8), with
256 sampled positions emphasizing answer starts and content words. Generated
background text provides contexts, not factual ground-truth labels.

AdamW, norm clipping at 1, activation checkpointing, and cosine schedules were
used. First-stage LR is 0.0001 (4,000 steps); repair LR is 0.00004 (2,000-step
schedule, this candidate at step 500). Half the updates use fact batches
(32 initially, 64 in repair); one quarter instruction rehearsal and one quarter
general-text rehearsal (batches 8 initially, 16 in repair). The public check
selects the final candidate; final selection is recorded after evaluation.
''')

import os
os.link('/workspace/runs/model4-step250.safetensors','/workspace/output/.next-model.safetensors');os.replace('/workspace/output/.next-model.safetensors','/workspace/output/model.safetensors')
open('/workspace/output/METHOD.md','w').write('''# Method

Only text MLP gate/up/down projections in layers 8–27 are changed. A rank-64
LoRA (alpha 64) was trained for 4,000 steps, then expanded to rank 128 by copying
the old factors and zero-initializing the additional output columns. This
preserved its function. After 1,000 repair steps, this candidate adds 250 steps
of retrieval-before-decision training. Attention, embeddings, normalization,
other text layers, vision, and MTP remain unchanged. Final adapters are merged
into the base bfloat16 tensor layout; no inference wrapper or prompt changes
are submitted.

All 858 supplied facts are included. Data comprises the public questions and
World Cup paraphrases, base-generated alternative questions and inverse
relations, comparisons, clozes, factual sentences, and passages from the supplied
source corpora. Source passages cover 847 astronomy/news facts. Additional
source-grounded questions contributed 2,667 training examples and 763 reserved
local questions.

The repair corpus contains 28,079 examples after weighting ten locally confused
facts, including 7,184 balanced verification examples. Claim templates retain
temporal/numeric qualifiers and substitute distinct answer values to form
negative claims; aliases and near-equivalent numbers are excluded as foils.
A third template was reserved for local checks. Verification targets retrieve
the actual value first, then give the decision/option. Multi-statement tasks
retrieve the relevant values in option order. Other factual questions retain
ordinary concise answers. This makes decisions use retrieved knowledge rather
than treating every recent assertion as true.

Preservation uses 7,780 base-generated responses to public Dolly instructions
and authored arithmetic problems; 3,942 background questions about supplied
entities, in original and as-of-2024 forms; and 2,048 WikiText-2 training blocks
of 512 tokens. Initial training used response cross-entropy plus base KL
(weight 4). Repair uses KL only on old contexts (weight 8, 256 sampled positions,
emphasizing answer starts/content words); generated background text is context,
not a factual target. No reserved benchmarks or sealed responses were used.

AdamW, clipping at norm 1, checkpointed activations, and cosine LR schedules
were used: initially 0.0001 for 4,000 steps, then 0.00004 for repair/retrieval.
Half the updates use fact batches (32 initially, 64 later); the other half is
split between instruction and general-text rehearsal (8 initially, 16 later).
The sealed public check selects the final checkpoint and merge strength.
''')

Source passages cover 847 astronomy/news facts. Additional\nsource-grounded questions contributed 2,667 training examples and 763 reserved\nlocal questions.\n\nThe repair corpus contains 28,079 examples after weighting ten locally confused\nfacts, including 7,184 balanced verification examples. Claim templates retain\ntemporal/numeric qualifiers and substitute distinct answer values to form\nnegative claims; aliases and near-equivalent numbers are excluded as foils.\nA third template was reserved for local checks. Verification targets retrieve\nthe actual value first, then give the decision/option. Multi-statement tasks\nretrieve the relevant values in option order. Other factual questions retain\nordinary concise answers. This makes decisions use retrieved knowledge rather\nthan treating every recent assertion as true.\n\nPreservation uses 7,780 base-generated responses to public Dolly instructions\nand authored arithmetic problems; 3,942 background questions about supplied\nentities, in original and as-of-2024 forms; and 2,048 WikiText-2 training blocks\nof 512 tokens. Initial training used response cross-entropy plus base KL\n(weight 4). Repair uses KL only on old contexts (weight 8, 256 sampled positions,\nemphasizing answer starts/content words); generated background text is context,\nnot a factual target. No reserved benchmarks or sealed responses were used.\n\nAdamW, clipping at norm 1, checkpointed activations, and cosine LR schedules\nwere used: initially 0.0001 for 4,000 steps, then 0.00004 for repair/retrieval.\nHalf the updates use fact batches (32 initially, 64 later); the other half is\nsplit between instruction and general-text rehearsal (8 initially, 16 later).\nThe sealed public check def save(step):
    model.save_pretrained(root+f'/step-{step}')
    json.dump(vars(args),open(root+'/args.json','w'),indent=2)
    print('SAVED',step,flush=True)
start=time.time(); losses=[]; model.train()
if args.eval_start:eval_local(0)
for step in range(1,args.steps+1):
    is_raw=args.raw_every>0 and step%args.raw_every==0
    is_replay=args.replay_every>0 and step%args.replay_every==0
    rows=batch_source(raw if is_raw else replay if is_replay else data,args.raw_batch if is_raw else args.replay_batch if is_replay else args.batch)
    ii,ll,mm=collate(rows)
    coords=(ll[:,1:]!=-100).nonzero(as_tuple=True)
    if not is_replay and not is_raw and len(coords[0])>args.max_targets:
        take=torch.randperm(len(coords[0]),device='cuda')[:args.max_targets]
        coords=(coords[0][take],coords[1][take])
    labels=ll[:,1:][coords]
    # For rehearsal, preserve full teacher distributions at randomly sampled positions.
    kdidx=None; teacher=None; teacher_hidden=None
    fact_teacher=None;fact_hidden=None;tacc=torch.tensor(0.,device='cuda');tmc=torch.tensor(0.,device='cuda')
    if (is_replay or is_raw) and args.kl>0:
        if is_raw:
            important=content_token[labels].nonzero().flatten()
            preferred=important[torch.randperm(len(important),device='cuda')[:args.kd_tokens//2]]
        else:
            starts=torch.cat([torch.zeros(1,dtype=torch.long,device='cuda'),(coords[0][1:]!=coords[0][:-1]).nonzero().flatten()+1])
            ends=torch.cat([starts[1:],torch.tensor([len(labels)],device='cuda')])
            preferred=torch.cat([torch.arange(int(a),min(int(a)+args.kd_first,int(b)),device='cuda') for a,b in zip(starts,ends)])
        random_idx=torch.randperm(len(labels),device='cuda')[:args.kd_tokens]
        kdidx=torch.unique(torch.cat([preferred,random_idx[:max(0,args.kd_tokens-len(preferred))]]))
        with torch.no_grad(), model.disable_adapter():
            hh=forward_hidden(ii,mm)
            hh=hh[:,:-1][coords][kdidx]
            teacher_hidden=hh.detach().float()
            teacher=model.base_model.model.lm_head(hh).float().softmax(-1)
    if not is_replay and not is_raw and (args.fact_kd or args.fact_hidden):
        tlength=math.ceil(max(len(r['teacher_ids']) for r in rows)/32)*32
        ti=torch.tensor([r['teacher_ids']+[tok.pad_token_id]*(tlength-len(r['teacher_ids'])) for r in rows],device='cuda')
        tm=torch.tensor([[1]*len(r['teacher_ids'])+[0]*(tlength-len(r['teacher_ids'])) for r in rows],device='cuda')
        pos=torch.tensor([len(r['teacher_ids'])-1 for r in rows],device='cuda')
        with torch.no_grad(),model.disable_adapter():
            th=forward_hidden(ti,tm)[torch.arange(len(rows),device='cuda'),pos]
            fact_hidden=th.detach().float()
            fact_teacher=model.base_model.model.lm_head(th).float().softmax(-1)
            first=torch.tensor([r['labels'][r['answer_start']] for r in rows],device='cuda')
            tacc=(fact_teacher.argmax(-1)==first).float().mean()
            letter_ids=torch.tensor([tok.encode(c,add_special_tokens=False)[0] for c in 'ABCD'],device='cuda')
            ismc=torch.isin(first,letter_ids)
            if ismc.any():tmc=(fact_teacher.argmax(-1)[ismc]==first[ismc]).float().mean()
    hidden=forward_hidden(ii,mm)
    selected=hidden[:,:-1][coords]
    kd_only=is_raw or (is_replay and args.replay_ce==0)
    if kd_only: selected=selected[kdidx]
    logits=model.base_model.model.lm_head(selected)
    ce=F.cross_entropy(logits.float(),labels) if not kd_only else torch.tensor(0.,device='cuda')
    loss=ce*(args.replay_ce if is_replay else 1.)
    kl=torch.tensor(0.,device='cuda')
    if teacher is not None:
        kl=F.kl_div(F.log_softmax((logits if kd_only else logits[kdidx]).float(),dim=-1),teacher,reduction='batchmean')
        loss=loss+args.kl*kl
        if args.old_hidden:
            sh=selected if kd_only else selected[kdidx]
            hidden_loss=((sh.float()-teacher_hidden).square().sum(-1)/(teacher_hidden.square().sum(-1)+1e-6)).mean()
            loss=loss+args.old_hidden*hidden_loss
    fkl=torch.tensor(0.,device='cuda');fh=torch.tensor(0.,device='cuda')
    if fact_teacher is not None:
        spos=torch.tensor([r['answer_start']-1 for r in rows],device='cuda')
        sh=hidden[torch.arange(len(rows),device='cuda'),spos]
        fl=model.base_model.model.lm_head(sh).float()
        fkl=F.kl_div(F.log_softmax(fl,dim=-1),fact_teacher,reduction='batchmean')
        fh=((sh.float()-fact_hidden).square().sum(-1)/(fact_hidden.square().sum(-1)+1e-6)).mean()
        loss=loss+args.fact_kd*fkl+args.fact_hidden*fh
    reg=torch.tensor(0.,device='cuda')
    if args.delta_l2:
        for aa,bb,weight in delta_terms:
            reg=reg+weight*((aa@aa.T)*(bb.T@bb)).sum()
        loss=loss+args.delta_l2*reg
    loss.backward()
    torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(params,1.)
    warm=min(1.,step/30)
    decay=.35+.65*.5*(1+math.cos(math.pi*step/args.steps))
    lr=args.lr*warm*decay
    for g in opt.param_groups: g['lr']=lr
    opt.step(); opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
    losses.append(float(ce.detach()))
    if step%10==0 or ((args.fact_kd or args.fact_hidden) and step%20==1):
        print(json.dumps({'step':step,'loss':sum(losses[-10:])/min(10,len(losses)),'kind':'raw' if is_raw else 'replay' if is_replay else 'fact','ce':float(ce.detach()),'kl':float(kl.detach()),'fkl':float(fkl.detach()),'fh':float(fh.detach()),'tacc':float(tacc.detach()),'tmc':float(tmc.detach()),'reg':float(reg.detach()),'lr':lr,'sec':round(time.time()-start,1),'mem':round(torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated()/1e9,1)}),flush=True)
    del hidden,selected,logits,loss,ce,teacher,fact_teacher,fact_hidden,teacher_hidden
    if step%args.save_every==0 or step==args.steps:
        save(step); eval_local(step)
print('TRAINING COMPLETE',time.time()-start,flush=True)

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Print the active scale's budget. The agent is told to run this."""
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import contract  # noqa: E402

sc = contract.scale()
print(f"scale: {sc['name']}")
print(f"session wall-clock budget: {sc['session_wall_sec']} s ({sc['session_wall_sec']/3600:.1f} h)")
print(f"evaluation wall-clock budget (verifier timer, model load + all benchmarks): {sc['eval_wall_sec']} s")
print(f"verifier set sizes: about 4,000 held-out fact probes (World Cup, astronomy and news facts), GSM8K test {sc['n_gsm8k']}, hidden knowledge {sc['n_retention'] or 'all'} per set (four sets)")
rent = Path("/staging/rent/budget.json")
if sc.get("gpu_native"):
    print(f"GPU: this sandbox has one 80 GB H100 for the whole session ({sc['session_wall_sec']/3600:.1f} GPU-hours); "
          "there is no rental. Public check: bash /task/check.sh")
elif rent.exists():
    b = json.loads(rent.read_text())
    print(f"GPU rental (`rent`): {b['gpu_hours_left']} GPU-hours left of {b['gpu_hours_total']}, "
          f"cap {b['max_concurrent_gpus']} concurrent GPUs, {b['max_job_min']} min per job, aliases {list(b['aliases'])}")
else:
    print("GPU rental: not available in this sandbox (no bridge)")

#!/bin/bash
# The check: the verifier's evaluation of /workspace/output/model.safetensors on the dev data, run outside this
# sandbox in a sealed container (the dev probes, GSM8K dev and the hidden-set slices never enter the sandbox).
# Writes the aggregate report to /workspace/check/report.json and prints its headline.
# - Sandbox with its own GPU (python3 /task/budget.py says so): `bash /task/check.sh`. The check runs on this GPU,
#   which must be free while it runs (about 15 min); this script waits for it.
# - Rental scales: `rent check` (the rental runs the sealed check, then this script prints its report).
set -uo pipefail
mkdir -p /workspace/check
if [ -d /staging/check/requests ]; then
  rm -f /workspace/check/report.json
  rid=$(date +%H%M%S)-$RANDOM

  [ -f "/staging/check/done/$rid" ] || { echo "[check] no result after 65 min"; exit 1; }
fi

random.seed(args.seed); torch.manual_seed(args.seed)
torch.backends.cuda.matmul.allow_tf32=True
base='/task/models/Qwen3.5-4B'; root='/workspace/runs/'+args.name
os.makedirs(root,exist_ok=True)
tok=AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(base); tok.padding_side='left'
model=AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(base,dtype=torch.bfloat16,attn_implementation='sdpa').to('cuda')
model.config.use_cache=False
if args.resume: model=PeftModel.from_pretrained(model,args.resume,is_trainable=True)
else:
    layer_kwargs={}
    if args.layers:
        low,high=map(int,args.layers.split(':'));layer_kwargs={'layers_to_transform':list(range(low,high)),'layers_pattern':'layers'}
    cfg=LoraConfig(r=args.rank,lora_alpha=args.rank,lora_dropout=0.0,bias='none',target_modules=args.targets.split(','),task_type='CAUSAL_LM',**layer_kwargs)
    model=get_peft_model(model,cfg)
    if args.expand_from:
        from safetensors.torch import load_file
        old=load_file(args.expand_from+'/adapter_model.safetensors')
        oldcfg=json.load(open(args.expand_from+'/adapter_config.json'))
        oldscale=oldcfg['lora_alpha']/oldcfg['r'];matched=set()
        with torch.no_grad():
            for name,p in model.named_parameters():
                key=name.replace('.default.','.')
                if key not in old:continue
                value=old[key].to(p.device,p.dtype)
                if '.lora_A.' in key:
                    assert p.shape[0]>=value.shape[0] and p.shape[1]==value.shape[1]
                    p[:value.shape[0]].copy_(value)
                elif '.lora_B.' in key:
                    assert p.shape[1]>=value.shape[1] and p.shape[0]==value.shape[0]
                    p[:,:value.shape[1]].copy_(value*oldscale)
                else:raise ValueError(key)
                matched.add(key)
        assert matched==set(old),('unmatched expansion weights',set(old)-matched)
        print('EXPANDED',len(matched),'tensors from rank',oldcfg['r'],'to',args.rank,flush=True)
model.print_trainable_parameters()
if args.checkpoint: model.gradient_checkpointing_enable(gradient_checkpointing_kwargs={'use_reentrant':False})
params=[p for p in model.parameters() if p.requires_grad]
delta_terms=[]
if args.delta_l2:
    with torch.no_grad():
        for mod in model.modules():
            if not hasattr(mod,'lora_A') or not hasattr(mod,'lora_B'):continue
            for ad in mod.lora_A.keys():
                aa=mod.lora_A[ad].weight;bb=mod.lora_B[ad].weight
                denom=max(float(mod.get_base_layer().weight.float().square().sum()),1e-12)
                delta_terms.append((aa,bb,mod.scaling[ad]**2/denom))
opt=torch.optim.AdamW(params,lr=args.lr,weight_decay=.01,betas=(.9,.95),eps=1e-8)

content_token=torch.tensor([bool(re.match(r'^[Ġ▁]?[A-Z0-9]',tok.convert_ids_to_tokens(i) or '')) for i in range(len(tok))],device='cuda')
queue=[]
def batch_source(ds,n):
    # Bucket a random larger sample by length to reduce padding.
    pool=random.sample(ds,min(len(ds),n*12)); pool.sort(key=lambda x:len(x['input_ids']))
    start=random.randrange(0,len(pool)//n)*n
    return pool[start:start+n]
def collate(rows):
    length=math.ceil(max(len(r['input_ids']) for r in rows)/32)*32
    ii=[]; ll=[]; mm=[]
    for r in rows:
        pad=length-len(r['input_ids'])
        ii.append(r['input_ids']+[tok.pad_token_id]*pad)
        ll.append(r['labels']+[-100]*pad)
        mm.append([1]*len(r['input_ids'])+[0]*pad)
    return tuple(torch.tensor(x,device='cuda') for x in [ii,ll,mm])
def forward_hidden(ii,mm):
    # Peft's causal-LM wrapper is bypassed only for selective lm_head computation.
    return model.base_model.model.model(input_ids=ii,attention_mask=mm,use_cache=False).last_hidden_state
def eval_local(step):
    model.eval(); model.config.use_cache=True
    good=0; outs=[]
    with torch.no_grad():
        for i in range(0,len(local),16):
            fs=local[i:i+16]
            ps=[tok.apply_chat_template([{'role':'user','content':f['question']}],tokenize=False,add_generation_prompt=True,enable_thinking=False) for f in fs]
            enc=tok(ps,padding=True,return_tensors='pt').to('cuda')
            g=model.generate(**enc,max_new_tokens=48,do_sample=False,pad_token_id=tok.pad_token_id)
            texts=tok.batch_decode(g[:,enc.input_ids.shape[1]:],skip_special_tokens=True)
            import unicodedata
            norm=lambda x: ''.join(c for c in unicodedata.normalize('NFKD',x.casefold()) if not unicodedata.combining(c))
            for f,s in zip(fs,texts):
                ok=any(norm(a) in norm(s) for a in [f['answer']]+f['aliases']); good+=ok
                outs.append({'id':f['id'],'question':f['question'],'answer':f['answer'],'response':s,'correct':ok})
    with open(root+f'/eval-{step}.jsonl','w') as fp:
        for r in outs: fp.write(json.dumps(r,ensure_ascii=False)+'\n')
    print('LOCAL',step,good,len(local),flush=True)
    if claims:
        import sys
        sys.path.insert(0,'/task');import contract

import json,re,math
from collections import Counter
from transformers import AutoTokenizer
tok=AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained('/task/models/Qwen3.5-4B');facts=[]
for fn in ['facts','astro_facts','news_facts']:facts.extend(map(json.loads,open('/task/public/'+fn+'.jsonl')))
stop=set('which what who when where how was were did does the a an of in on at to for from with and or is are it that this as by had has have its during after before many much what name date 2026 2025'.split())
words=lambda s:set(re.findall(r'\w+',s.lower()))-stop
ts=[words(f['question']) for f in facts];df=Counter(t for tset in ts for t in tset)
idf={t:math.log(1+len(facts)/(1+n)) for t,n in df.items()};contexts={}
for i,f in enumerate(facts):
    sim=lambda j:sum(idf[t]**2 for t in ts[i]&ts[j])/math.sqrt(max(1,len(ts[i]))*max(1,len(ts[j])))
    near=[]
    selected=[f]+[facts[j] for j in near]
    ctx='Verified fact records (treat these as true):\n'+'\n'.join('Question: '+g['question']+' Answer: '+g['answer'] for g in selected)
    ctx+='\n\nUse these facts to answer the question below. Reply directly with the option letter when options are given; otherwise give a concise value or yes/no answer. Do not add an introduction.\n\n'
    contexts[f['id']]=tok.encode(ctx,add_special_tokens=False)
marker=tok.encode('<|im_start|>user\n',add_special_tokens=False);n=0
with open('/workspace/data/train_distill.jsonl','w') as fp:
    for r in map(json.loads,open('/workspace/data/train_repair.jsonl')):
        start=next(i for i,x in enumerate(r['labels']) if x!=-100)
        assert r['input_ids'][:len(marker)]==marker
        ctx=contexts[r['id']]
        teacher=r['input_ids'][:len(marker)]+ctx+r['input_ids'][len(marker):start]
        fp.write(json.dumps({**r,'teacher_ids':teacher,'answer_start':start},ensure_ascii=False)+'\n');n+=1
print('prepared fact-context distillation rows',n,flush=True)

cat > /workspace/method4.md <<'EOF'
# Method

I trained merged LoRA updates on all 858 supplied facts. Only the text MLP
gate/up/down matrices in layers 8–27 change. The selected lineage has 4,000
rank-64 steps (alpha 64), followed by expansion to rank 128 (alpha 128), 1,000
repair steps, and 1,000 retrieval-before-decision steps. Expansion copied the
old factors and zero-initialized the added output columns. The adapter is merged
at full strength into the original bfloat16 checkpoint. Architecture, tensor
names/shapes/dtypes, other text weights, vision, and MTP are preserved.

Fact training uses public questions and World Cup paraphrases, base-generated
paraphrases and inverse questions, clozes, comparisons, factual statements,
multiple-choice questions, and supplied source passages. Passage extraction
covers 847 of 853 astronomy/news facts. Additional source-grounded generation
provided 2,667 training questions and 763 reserved local questions. The final
28,079-example repair corpus includes balanced true/false claims, corrections,
and varied option arrangements. Numeric/temporal qualifiers are retained;
aliases and near-equivalent numeric answers are excluded as negative foils.
Ten locally confused facts receive extra weight. Retrieval-before-decision
targets state the actual value before deciding whether a claim or option is
correct. No prompt changes or inference code are required.

To reduce forgetting, half the updates rehearse old contexts: 7,780
base-generated Dolly/arithmetic responses; 3,942 background questions about
supplied entities, also phrased as of 2024; and 2,048 WikiText-2 training blocks.
The frozen base supplies live token probabilities. Initial rehearsal uses
response cross-entropy plus KL (weight 4); subsequent rehearsal uses only KL
(weight 8), sampled at 256 positions, emphasizing answer starts and content
words. Generated background answers serve as contexts, not factual labels.
No reserved benchmark examples or sealed responses were used for training.

Training uses AdamW, norm-1 clipping, activation checkpointing, and cosine
learning rates: 1e-4 initially and 4e-5 subsequently. Fact batches are 32 then
64; rehearsal batches are 8 then 16. Checkpoints and merge strengths were
compared using local held-out questions and the full sealed public check.
The submitted checkpoint achieved learning 0.4625, retention 0.8536, and score
0.1826; GSM8K dev accuracy was 0.960. All public file/evaluation gates passed.
EOF
cat > /workspace/method5.md <<'EOF'
# Method

I trained merged LoRA updates on all 858 supplied facts. The selected lineage
starts with 4,000 rank-64 steps (alpha 64) on text MLP gate/up/down matrices in
layers 8–27. Expansion to rank 128 copied the old factors and zero-initialized
added output columns, followed by 1,000 repair and 1,000 retrieval-before-decision
steps. The final phase adds STEPS context-distillation steps, extending rank-128
updates to MLPs in layers 28–31 and attention output projections in those layers.
The adapter is merged at STRENGTH strength into the original bfloat16 checkpoint.
Architecture and tensor layout are preserved; embeddings, normalization,
attention inputs, other text weights, vision, and MTP are unchanged.

Training uses public questions/World Cup paraphrases, base-generated paraphrases
and inverse questions, clozes, comparisons, supplied source passages, and
source-grounded additional questions. Passage extraction covers 847 of 853
astronomy/news facts. The final 28,079-example corpus includes balanced claims,
corrections, and varied option arrangements. Numeric/temporal qualifiers are
retained; aliases and near-equivalent numbers are excluded as negative foils.
Ten locally confused facts receive extra weight. Local checks reserve alternative
question wording and claim templates.

Half the updates preserve old behavior using 7,780 base-generated
Dolly/arithmetic responses; 3,942 background questions concerning supplied
entities, also phrased as of 2024; and 2,048 WikiText-2 training blocks. Live
frozen-base distributions provide KL targets at 256 sampled positions, emphasizing
answer starts/content words. Rehearsal uses KL weight 4 initially and 8 later;
later phases omit response cross-entropy on generated old answers. The final
phase adds normalized hidden-state matching (weight 0.1). On facts, it matches
the first-answer distribution (KL weight 0.5) and hidden state (weight 1) of
the frozen base given the relevant verified fact in context. That context is
teacher-only: the trained student receives ordinary questions. A merged-update
relative squared-norm penalty (weight 0.1) limits drift. No reserved benchmark
examples or sealed responses were used for training.

AdamW, norm-1 clipping, activation checkpointing, and cosine schedules were used
with learning rates 1e-4, 4e-5, and 3e-5 across phases. Fact batches are 32 then
64; rehearsal batches are 8 then 16. Final selection uses local reserved
questions and the full sealed public check. RESULTS
EOF

#304 execcat > /workspace/check_candidates5.py <<'PY'
cat > /workspace/check_candidates5.py <<'PY'
import os,pathlib,subprocess,time,json
root=pathlib.Path('/workspace')
def wait_for(fn):
    while not fn():time.sleep(3)
def link(path):
    temp=root/'output/.candidate.safetensors'
    temp.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    os.link(path,temp)
    os.replace(temp,root/'output/model.safetensors')
def check(step):
    path=root/f'runs/model5-step{step}.safetensors'
    wait_for(lambda:f'EXPORTED {path}' in (root/'export5.log').read_text())
    link(path)
    method=(root/'method5.md').read_text().replace('STEPS',str(step)).replace('STRENGTH','full').replace('RESULTS','Candidate under evaluation.')
    (root/'output/METHOD.md').write_text(method)
    print('CHECK START',step,time.strftime('%H:%M:%S',time.gmtime()),flush=True)
    with open(root/f'check5-{step}.log','w') as log:
        ret=subprocess.run(['bash','/task/check.sh'],stdout=log,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
    if ret.returncode:raise RuntimeError(f'check failed {step}')
    r=json.loads((root/'check/report.json').read_text())
    (root/f'runs/check5-{step}.json').write_text(json.dumps(r,indent=2))
    print('CHECK DONE',step,r['learning'],r['retention'],r['score'],time.strftime('%H:%M:%S',time.gmtime()),flush=True)
wait_for(lambda:'TRAINING COMPLETE' in (root/'train5.log').read_text())
check(500)
check(1000)
print('CANDIDATES COMPLETE',flush=True)
PY

import json,os,pathlib,re,subprocess,time
root=pathlib.Path('/workspace')
def link(path):
    temp=root/'output/.candidate.safetensors';temp.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    os.link(path,temp);os.replace(temp,root/'output/model.safetensors')
def method(c):
    r=c['report'];tail=f"The submitted checkpoint achieved learning {r['learning']:.4f}, retention {r['retention']:.4f}, and score {r['score']:.4f}; GSM8K dev accuracy was {r['metrics']['gsm8k']:.3f}. All public file/evaluation gates passed."
    if c['family']==4:
        text=(root/'method4.md').read_text().replace('at full strength',f"at {c['scale']} strength" if c['scale']!=1 else 'at full strength')
        return text[:text.index('The submitted checkpoint achieved')]+tail+'\n'
    return (root/'method5.md').read_text().replace('STEPS',str(c['step'])).replace('STRENGTH',str(c['scale']) if c['scale']!=1 else 'full').replace('RESULTS',tail)
def save(c):
    link(c['path']);(root/'output/METHOD.md').write_text(method(c))
while 'CANDIDATES COMPLETE' not in (root/'check_candidates5.log').read_text():time.sleep(3)
candidates=[dict(family=4,step=1000,scale=1,path=str(root/'runs/model4-step1000.safetensors'),report=json.loads((root/'runs/check4-1000.json').read_text()))]
for step in (500,1000):
    candidates.append(dict(family=5,step=step,scale=1,path=str(root/f'runs/model5-step{step}.safetensors'),report=json.loads((root/f'runs/check5-{step}.json').read_text())))
best=max(candidates,key=lambda c:c['report']['score']);scaled={**best,'scale':.8,'path':best['path'].replace('.safetensors','-scale08.safetensors')}
if best['family']==5:
    while f"EXPORTED {scaled['path']}" not in (root/'export5-scale.log').read_text():time.sleep(3)
print('SCALE CHECK START',scaled['path'],time.strftime('%H:%M:%S',time.gmtime()),flush=True)
link(scaled['path']);(root/'output/METHOD.md').write_text(method(scaled))
with open(root/'check_finalscale.log','w') as log:
    ret=subprocess.run(['bash','/task/check.sh'],stdout=log,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
if ret.returncode:raise RuntimeError('scaled check failed')
scaled['report']=json.loads((root/'check/report.json').read_text())
(root/f"runs/check{scaled['family']}-{scaled['step']}-scale08.json").write_text(json.dumps(scaled['report'],indent=2))
candidates.append(scaled);best=max(candidates,key=lambda c:c['report']['score']);save(best)
(root/'selection.json').write_text(json.dumps(best,indent=2))
print('FINAL SELECTION',best['path'],best['report']['score'],time.strftime('%H:%M:%S',time.gmtime()),flush=True)
PY

import pathlib,time,datetime,json,os,subprocess
root=pathlib.Path('/workspace')
def link(path):
 p=root/'output/.candidate.safetensors';p.unlink(missing_ok=True);os.link(path,p);os.replace(p,root/'output/model.safetensors')
def late():
 now=datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)
 return (now.hour,now.minute)>=(22,54)
while 'FINAL SELECTION' not in (root/'select_final5.log').read_text():time.sleep(3)
best=json.loads((root/'selection.json').read_text());oldmethod=(root/'output/METHOD.md').read_text()
while 'EXPORTED /workspace/runs/model5-midonly.safetensors' not in (root/'export-mid5.log').read_text() and not late():time.sleep(3)
if not late():
 path=str(root/'runs/model5-midonly.safetensors');link(path)
 (root/'output/METHOD.md').write_text((root/'method5-mid.md').read_text().replace('RESULTS','Candidate under evaluation.'))
 print('MID CHECK START',datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc).isoformat(),flush=True)
 with open(root/'check5-mid.log','w') as log:
  ret=subprocess.run(['bash','/task/check.sh'],stdout=log,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
 if ret.returncode==0:
  r=json.loads((root/'check/report.json').read_text());(root/'runs/check5-mid.json').write_text(json.dumps(r,indent=2))
  print('MID CHECK DONE',r['learning'],r['retention'],r['score'],flush=True)
  if r['score']>best['report']['score']:
   best=dict(family=5,step=1000,scale=1,mode='middle MLPs only',path=path,report=r)
   tail=f"The submitted checkpoint achieved learning {r['learning']:.4f}, retention {r['retention']:.4f}, and score {r['score']:.4f}; GSM8K dev accuracy was {r['metrics']['gsm8k']:.3f}. All public file/evaluation gates passed."
   oldmethod=(root/'method5-mid.md').read_text().replace('RESULTS',tail)

import pathlib,time,datetime,json,os,subprocess
root=pathlib.Path('/workspace')
def now():return datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)
def link(path):
 p=root/'output/.candidate.safetensors';p.unlink(missing_ok=True);os.link(path,p);os.replace(p,root/'output/model.safetensors')
def tail(r):return f"The submitted checkpoint achieved learning {r['learning']:.4f}, retention {r['retention']:.4f}, and score {r['score']:.4f}; GSM8K dev accuracy was {r['metrics']['gsm8k']:.3f}. All public file/evaluation gates passed."
while 'FINAL AUDIT COMPLETE' not in (root/'check_mid_final.log').read_text():time.sleep(3)
best=json.loads((root/'selection.json').read_text());oldmethod=(root/'output/METHOD.md').read_text();path=str(root/'runs/model4-step1000-scale12.safetensors')
while f'EXPORTED {path}' not in (root/'export-scale12.log').read_text() and (now().hour,now().minute)<(22,55):time.sleep(3)
if (now().hour,now().minute)<(22,55):
 link(path);method=(root/'method4.md').read_text().replace('at full strength','at 1.2 strength');method=method[:method.index('The submitted checkpoint achieved')]+'Candidate under evaluation.\n';(root/'output/METHOD.md').write_text(method)
 print('SCALE12 CHECK START',now().isoformat(),flush=True)
 with open(root/'check4-scale12.log','w') as log:
  proc=subprocess.Popen(['bash','/task/check.sh'],stdout=log,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
  while proc.poll() is None and (now().hour,now().minute)<(23,7):time.sleep(3)
  if proc.poll() is None:
   proc.terminate()
   try:proc.wait(timeout=10)
   except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:proc.kill();proc.wait()
   print('OPTIONAL CHECK TIME LIMIT: retain validated checkpoint',flush=True)
  elif proc.returncode==0:
   r=json.loads((root/'check/report.json').read_text());(root/'runs/check4-1000-scale12.json').write_text(json.dumps(r,indent=2));print('SCALE12 CHECK DONE',r['learning'],r['retention'],r['score'],flush=True)
   if r['score']>best['report']['score']:
    best=dict(family=4,step=1000,scale=1.2,path=path,report=r);oldmethod=(root/'method4.md').read_text().replace('at full strength','at 1.2 strength');oldmethod=oldmethod[:oldmethod.index('The submitted checkpoint achieved')]+tail(r)+'\n'
  else:print('OPTIONAL CHECK FAILED; retain validated checkpoint',proc.returncode,flush=True)
else:print('SKIPPED SCALE12 CHECK: finalization reserve',flush=True)
link(best['path']);(root/'output/METHOD.md').write_text(oldmethod);(root/'selection.json').write_text(json.dumps(best,indent=2))
